, respectively.

C.1
Additional results

We first establish several basic properties of the stationary AR(1)–MSBM, which will be used throughout the analysis.

Proposition 6
.

Let the process
\{\mathbf{A}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset\{0,1\}^{n\times n\times L}
be defined in
Definition

1
. Then it is a strictly stationary process. Moreover, for any
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
,
l\in[L]
and
t,s\geq 0
, we have

\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l}^{t}=\mathbb{P}\{\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t}=1\}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}},\quad\mathrm{Var}(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t})=\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})^{2}},

and

\rho_{i,j,l}(|t-s|)=\mathrm{Corr}(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t},\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{s})=(1-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})^{|t-s|}.

The proof follows directly from the same argument as Proposition 2 in
Jiang et al. 2023
and is omitted.

To analyze temporal dependence, we recall the notion of strong mixing, which quantifies the decay of dependence across time. The following lemma establishes that each edgewise process in the stationary AR(1)-MSBM is strongly mixing.

Definition 6

(Strongly mixing)
.

Let
\{X_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
be a stochastic process.
The
\alpha
-mixing coefficient at lag
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
is defined as

\alpha(k)=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}\sup_{\mathcal{A}\in\mathcal{F}_{0}^{t},\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{F}_{t+k}^{\infty}}|\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{B})-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B})|,

where
\mathcal{F}_{0}^{t}=\sigma(X_{0},X_{1},\cdots,X_{t})
is the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the process up to time
t
and
\mathcal{F}_{t+k}^{\infty}=\sigma(X_{t+k},X_{t+k+1},\cdots)
is the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the process from time
t+k
onward. The process
\{X_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is is said to be strongly mixing if

\alpha(k)\to 0\quad\mbox{as}\quad k\to\infty.

Lemma 7
.

Let the process
\{\mathbf{A}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset\{0,1\}^{n\times n\times L}
be defined in
Definition

1
. For any
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
and
l\in[L]
, the univariate edge process
\{\mathbf{A}^{t}_{i,j,l}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset\{0,1\}^{n\times n\times L}
is strongly mixing.

The proof of
Lemma

7
follows the same argument as that of
Lemma

13
and is in fact simpler; we omit the details for brevity.

We next establish theoretical guarantees for the initial estimators of the transition probabilities,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{t}
, corresponding to
Stage I
of
Algorithm

1
. This result extends the single-layer analysis in Proposition 6 of
Jiang et al. 2023
to the multilayer setting.

Proposition 8
.

Let the process
\{\mathbf{A}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset\{0,1\}^{n\times n\times L}
be defined in
Definition

1
. For any
t\in[T]
with
t\{\log(t)\log\log(t)\}^{-2}\gtrsim\log(n\vee L\vee T)
, there exist absolute constants
C,c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|+\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}\big|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}},\forall 1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c}.

Moreover, for any
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
,
l\in[L]
and
t\in[T]
, both
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}
and
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}
are
C^{*}t^{-1/2}
-sub-Gaussian distributed for some absolute constant
C^{*}>0
.

We next study spectral properties of the transition probability tensors. Define

U_{Z}=ZD_{Z}^{-1},\quad\mbox{where }D_{Z}=\mbox{diag}(\sqrt{s_{1}},\dots,\sqrt{s_{K}}),

(25)

and let the singular value decomposition of
\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{W})
be

\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{W})=U_{W}D_{W}V_{W}^{\top}
,
with
U_{W}\in\mathbb{O}_{L\times r_{1}}
,
V_{W}\mathbb{O}_{n^{2}\times r_{1}}
and
D_{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{1}}
. Let
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K\times r_{1}}
satisfy

\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{S})=D_{W}V_{W}^{\top}
,
and define

\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{S}\times_{1}D_{Z}\times_{2}D_{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K\times r_{1}}.

Under
1
(i)
, the transition probability tensor
\boldsymbol{\Theta}
admits the Tucker decomposition

\boldsymbol{\Theta}=\mathbf{R}\times_{1}U_{Z}\times_{2}U_{Z}\times_{3}U_{W}\quad\mbox{with Tucker ranks }(K,K,r_{1}),

(26)

and an analogous representation holds for
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
.

Lemma 9
.

Let
\mathbf{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K\times L}
be defined in
1
and let
\boldsymbol{\Omega}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times L}
be given by
\boldsymbol{\Omega}=\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}
. Assume that
\mathrm{rank}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})\big)=K
and let
r=\mathrm{rank}\big(\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{Q})\big)
. Then, we have that

\mathrm{rank}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)=\mathrm{rank}\big(\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)=K,\quad\mathrm{rank}\big(\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)=r,

and

s_{\min}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{Q})\leq\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathcal{M}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\Omega})\big)\leq\big\|\mathcal{M}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\Omega})\big\|\leq s_{\max}\|\mathbf{Q}\|,\quad\forall s\in[3],

where
s_{\min}=\min_{k\in[K]}s_{k}
and
s_{\max}=\max_{k\in[K]}s_{k}
with for any
k\in[K]
,
s_{k}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,k}
.

Under
1
, the estimated subspace
\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}
obtained via H-PCA (
Stage II
of
Algorithm

1
) satisfies the following bound.

Proposition 10
.

Let the process
\{\mathbf{A}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset\{0,1\}^{n\times n\times L}
be defined in
Definition

1
. Suppose that
1
holds.
Let
U_{Z}
be defined in (
25
). For any
t\in[T]
, under
Algorithm

1
, with probability at least
1-(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c}
,

\inf_{O\in\mathbb{O}_{K\times K}}\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O\|\leq\mathcal{E}_{t}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{t}=C\sigma_{Q}^{-2}\bigg(\frac{K^{2}\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}+\frac{K^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg)+C\sigma_{Q}^{-1}\bigg(K\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}}+\frac{K\sqrt{L}}{t}\bigg),

(27)

with
\sigma_{Q}=\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{Q})
and absolute constants
C,c>0
.

When
K
is treated as a constant and
\sigma_{Q}\asymp\sqrt{L}
, the bound in (
27
) simplifies (up to logarithmic factors) to

\frac{1}{\sqrt{tnL}}+\frac{1}{t}.

The first term reflects stochastic variability, while the second captures the bias inherent to the initial estimators, which are maximum likelihood estimators (MLEs) of the autoregressive model.
For comparison, the single-layer result in Theorem 10 od
Jiang et al. 2023
achieved an error rate of order

\frac{1}{\sqrt{tn}}+\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{n}.

Our multilayer setting yields a stochastic term that scales as
(tnL)^{-1/2}
, reflecting the additional information aggregated across
L
layers. Moreover, our approach does not incur the extra
n^{-1}
bias term present in
Jiang et al. 2023
, since we perform spectral estimation directly on the initial estimators instead on a normalized Laplacian matrix, thereby avoiding the bias induced by diagonal normalization.

Finally, a key ingredient for proving the minimax lower bound in
Proposition

2
is a packing argument over the space of community labels. To construct the required packing set, we establish
Lemma

11
, whose proof relies on
Lemma

12
.

Lemma 11
.

Let
k\geq 2
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
. A labeling is a function
Z:[n]\to[k]
that assigns one of the
k
labels to each of the
n
indices.
Let
q=\lfloor n/k\rfloor
and
r=n-kq
. Define
\mathcal{Z}
to be the collection of all balanced labelings, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}=\Big\{Z\colon[n]\to[k]\Big|

\displaystyle\big|\{i\in[n]:Z(i)=a\}\big|\in\{q,q+1\},\forall a\in[k],

\displaystyle\mbox{ and }\big|\big\{a\in[k]\colon|\{i\in[n]:Z(i)=a\}|=q+1\big\}\big|=r\Big\}.

Then there exist absolute constants
c,c^{\prime}>0

and a subset
\mathcal{Z}^{*}\subset\mathcal{Z}
such that

|\mathcal{Z}^{*}|\geq\exp\{cn\log(k)\}\quad\mbox{and}\quad d_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})\geq c^{\prime}n,\quad\forall Z\neq Z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}^{*},

where
d_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})=|\{i\in[n]:Z(i)\neq Z^{\prime}(i)\}|
is the Hamming distance.

Lemma 12
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
0<\delta<1/2
, it holds that

\sum_{s=0}^{\lfloor\delta n\rfloor}\binom{n}{s}\leq\exp\big\{nH(\delta)\big\},\quad\mbox{where }H(\delta)=-\delta\log\delta-(1-\delta)\log(1-\delta).

C.2
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof of Theorem
Theorem

1
.

Recall that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}\times_{1}\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z})^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z})^{\top}\times_{3}\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{W})^{\top}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{1}\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z})^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z})^{\top}\times_{3}\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{W})^{\top}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\big\|(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta})\times_{1}\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z})^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z})^{\top}\times_{3}\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}(\widehat{U}^{t}_{W})^{\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I)+(II).

(28)

Step 1.
In this step, we first consider the term
(I)
in (
C.2
). Observe that

\displaystyle(I)=

\displaystyle\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}}+\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W\perp}}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W\perp}}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{W\perp}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I.1)+(I.2)+(I,3).

(29)

For the term
(I.1)
, we have that

\displaystyle(I.1)=

\displaystyle\big\|(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp})^{\top}U_{Z}U_{Z}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\big\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z\perp}U_{Z}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big\|U_{Z}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z},U_{Z}^{\top})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\|\leq s_{\max}\big\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z},U_{Z}^{\top})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{W}\|,

(30)

where the first inequity follows from
Lemma

9
.
Similarly, we obtain that

\displaystyle(I.2)\leq s_{\max}\big\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z},U_{Z}^{\top})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{W}\|\quad\mbox{and}\quad(I.3)\leq s_{\max}\big\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{t}_{W},U_{W}^{\top})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\mathbf{W}\|.

(31)

Under
1
, by
Proposition

10
we have that

\mathbb{P}\big\{\big\|\sin\Theta\big(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z},U_{Z})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}>\mathcal{E}_{t}\big\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{1}},

(32)

where

\mathcal{E}_{t}=C_{1}\sigma^{-2}\bigg(\frac{K^{2}\sqrt{K}\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}+\frac{K^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg)+C_{1}\sigma^{-1}\bigg(K\sqrt{\frac{K\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}}+\frac{K\sqrt{L}}{t}\bigg),

with absolute constants
C_{1},c_{1}>0
.
Similarly, under
1
, we have that

\mathbb{P}\big\{\big\|\sin\Theta\big(\widehat{U}^{t}_{W},U_{W})\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}>\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\prime}\big\}\geq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{1}},

(33)

where

\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\prime}=C_{1}\sigma^{-2}\bigg(\frac{K^{2}\sqrt{Lr}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}+\frac{K^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg)+C_{1}\sigma^{-1}\bigg(K\sqrt{\frac{Lr\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn^{2}}}+\frac{K\sqrt{L}}{t}\bigg).

Combining (
C.2
), (
C.2
), (
31
), (
32
) and (
33
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(I)>C_{2}\sigma^{-1}\bigg(\frac{K\sqrt{(K\vee r)L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}+\frac{KLn}{t^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+C_{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(Lr+nK)\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+\frac{\sqrt{L}n}{t}\bigg)\bigg\}\geq 2(n\vee L\vee T)^{c},

(34)

where
C_{2}>0
is an absolute constant.

Step 2.

In this step, we focus on the term
(II)
in (
C.2
). Note that

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times L}\\
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\big\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta},\mathbf{V}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times L}\\
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\big\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}-\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}\}+\boldsymbol{\Theta},\mathbf{V}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times L}\\
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\big\langle\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}\}-\boldsymbol{\Theta},\mathbf{V}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(II.1)+(II.2).

(35)

For the term
(II.1)
in (
C.2
), by
Lemma E.5 in the Supplement of
Han et al. 2022
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(II.1)>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(K^{2}r_{1}+nK+Lr_{1})\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{3}},

(36)

where
C_{3},c_{3}>0
are absolute constants.
For the term
(II.2)
in (
C.2
), we can derive for some absolute constant
C_{4}>0
that

\displaystyle(II.2)\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}\}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times L}\\
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\big\|\mathbf{V}\times_{1}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{2}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}}\times_{3}P_{\widehat{U}^{t}_{W}}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C_{4}\frac{n\sqrt{L}}{t},

(37)

where the last inequality follows from (
107
).
Combining (
C.2
), (
36
), (
37
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(II)>C_{3}\sqrt{\frac{(K^{2}r_{1}+nK+Lr_{1})\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+C_{4}\frac{n\sqrt{L}}{t}\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c}.

(38)

Step 3.

Combining (
C.2
), (
C.2
) and (
38
), we obtain for some absolute constants
C_{5},c_{5}>0
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}>C_{5}\sigma^{-1}\bigg(\frac{K\sqrt{(K\vee r)L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}+\frac{KLn}{t^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+C_{5}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(nK+Lr+K^{2}r)\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+\frac{\sqrt{L}n}{t}\bigg)\big\}\geq(n\vee L\vee T)^{c_{5}}.

(39)

Similarly, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{F}}>C_{5}\sigma^{-1}\bigg(\frac{K\sqrt{(K\vee r)L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}+\frac{KLn}{t^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+C_{5}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{(nK+Lr+K^{2}r)\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+\frac{\sqrt{L}n}{t}\bigg)\big\}\geq(n\vee L\vee T)^{c_{5}}.

(40)

Combining (
C.2
) and (
C.2
), we complete the proof.

∎

C.3
Proof of Proposition
2

Proof.

The proof proceeds in five main steps. Throughout, for any
(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})\in\mathcal{P}
, let
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}
denote the path distribution of the adjacency tensor sequence
\{\mathbf{A}^{t}\}_{t\in[T]\cup\{0\}}
generated by
Definition

1
with transition probabilities
(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})
.

Step 1: Kullback–Leibler(KL) divergence control.
Fix
(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta}),(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}
.
In this step, we compute the KL divergence between the path distributions induced by
(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})
and
(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})
.

The chain rule for KL divergence yields that

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\big(\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}(\mathbf{A}^{0})\big\|\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})}(\mathbf{A}^{0})\big)+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big\{\mathrm{KL}\big(\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}(\mathbf{A}^{t}\mid\mathbf{A}^{t-1})\big\|\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})}(\mathbf{A}^{t}\mid\mathbf{A}^{t-1})\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\mathrm{KL}\Big(\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{0}\big)\Big\|\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{0}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big\{\mathrm{KL}\Big(\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t}\mid\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t-1}\big)\Big\|\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t}\mid\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t-1}\big)\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I.1)+(I.2),

(41)

where

•

\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}(\mathbf{A}^{0})
is the marginal distribution
of
\mathbf{A}^{0}
under the parameters
(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})
;

•

\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}(\mathbf{A}^{t}\mid\mathbf{A}^{t-1})
is the conditional distribution of
\mathbf{A}^{t}
given
\mathbf{A}^{t-1}
under the parameters
(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})
;

•

\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}(\mathbf{A}^{0}_{i,j,l})
is the marginal distribution
of the edge variable
\mathbf{A}^{0}_{i,j,l}
under the parameters
(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})
; and

•

\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}(\mathbf{A}^{t}_{i,j,l}\mid\mathbf{A}^{t-1}_{i,j,l})

is the conditional distribution of
\mathbf{A}^{t}_{i,j,l}
given its previous state
\mathbf{A}^{t-1}_{i,j,l}
under the parameters
(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})
.

Step 1.1.
In this sub-step, we analyze the term
(I.1)
in (
C.3
).

By Lemma 15 in
Cai et al. 2024
, we have that

\displaystyle(I.1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\mathrm{KL}\Big(\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{0}\big)\Big\|\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{0}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\mathrm{KL}\bigg(\mbox{Bernoulli}\bigg(\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}\bigg)\Big\|\mbox{Bernoulli}\bigg(\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\frac{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}\bigg(\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\frac{1}{c_{\min}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}\bigg)^{2},

(42)

where the last inequality follows from
\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta},\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{n\times n\times L}
.

Let
g:[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{2}\to[0,1]
de defined by

g(\theta,\delta)=\frac{\theta}{\theta+\delta}.

It follows that

\|\nabla g(\theta,\delta)\|_{2}^{2}=\bigg\|\bigg(\frac{\delta}{(\theta+\delta)^{2}},-\frac{\theta}{(\theta+\delta)^{2}}\bigg)\bigg\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{\delta^{2}}{(\theta+\delta)^{4}}+\frac{\theta^{2}}{(\theta+\delta)^{4}}\leq\frac{2}{c_{\min}^{4}}.

(43)

Fix
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
and
l\in[L]
. By the mean value theorem, there exists a point

(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l})

on the line segment between

(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})
and

(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})
such that

\displaystyle\big|g(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})-g(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})\big|=

\displaystyle\nabla g(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l})^{\top}\big(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l})\|_{2}\sqrt{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})^{2}+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{c_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})^{2}+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})^{2}},

(44)

where the first inequality follows from Cauchy–Schwartz inequality and the last inequality follows from (
43
).
Combining (
C.3
) and (
C.3
) yields that

\displaystyle(I.1)\leq 2c_{\min}^{-6}\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big).

(45)

Step 1.2.
In this sub-step, we analyze the term
(I.2)
in (
C.3
).

By (
1
), we obtain that

\displaystyle(I.2)=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big\{\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t-1}=1\big)\mathrm{KL}\Big(\mbox{Bernoulli}\big(1-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}\big)\Big\|\mbox{Bernoulli}\big(1-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\pi_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l},\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l})}\big(\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t-1}=0\big)\mathrm{KL}\Big(\mbox{Bernoulli}\big(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big)\Big\|\mbox{Bernoulli}\big(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}\big)\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bigg\{\frac{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}\frac{\big(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}\big)^{2}}{(1-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}+\frac{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}{\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}}\frac{\big(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}\big)^{2}}{(1-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}^{\prime}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{T}{c_{\min}^{3}}\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big),

(46)

where the first inequality follows from Lemma 15 in
Cai et al. 2024
and the last inequality follows from
\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta},\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{n\times n\times L}
.

Step 1.3.
Combining (
C.3
), (
45
) and (
C.3
), we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime})}\big)\leq\big(2c_{\min}^{-6}+Tc_{\min}^{-3}\big)\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big).

(47)

Step 2: Connectivity probability construction.

In this step, we construct a lower bound by fixing a balanced community membership matrix
Z
and varying the connectivity tensors
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{M}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{K\times K\times L}
with
\max\{\mbox{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{W}),\mbox{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{M})\}\leq r
.

By the Varshamov–Gilbert Lemma, there exists a collection of tensors
\{\mathbf{S}^{(m)}\}_{m=1}^{M}\subset\{0,1\}^{K\times K\times r}
such that for some absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq K,l\in[r]}\mathbbm{1}\Big\{\big(\mathbf{S}^{(m)}\big)_{i,j,l}\neq\big(\mathbf{S}^{(m^{\prime})}\big)_{i,j,l}\Big\}\geq c_{1}rK^{2},\quad\forall m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]

(48)

and

M\geq 2^{c_{2}K^{2}r}.

(49)

For each
m\in[M]
, define

\mathbf{H}^{(m)}=\mathbf{H}^{(0)}+\delta\mathbf{S}^{(m)}\in[0,1]^{K\times K\times r},\quad\mbox{where }\big(\mathbf{H}^{(0)}\big)_{i,j,l}=\frac{1}{2},\quad\forall 1\leq i\leq j\leq K,l\in[r].

(50)

Next, partition
[L]
into
r
disjoint subsets
G_{1},\ldots,G_{r}
with

\big||G_{s}|-L/r\big|\leq 1,\quad\forall s\in[r].

Define
U\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times r}
by

U_{l,s}=\begin{cases}1,&l\in G_{s},\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}\quad\forall l\in[L],s\in[r].

Then

U^{\top}U=\mathrm{diag}\big(|G_{1}|,\ldots,|G_{r}|\big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lfloor L/r\rfloor I_{r}\preceq U^{\top}U\preceq\lceil L/r\rceil I_{r}.

(51)

Now define

\mathbf{W}^{(m)}=\mathbf{H}^{(m)}\times_{3}U,\quad\mathbf{M}^{(m)}=\mathbf{H}^{(m)}\times_{3}U,

(52)

and

\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}=\mathbf{W}^{(m)}\times_{1}Z\times_{2}Z,\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}=\mathbf{M}^{(m)}\times_{1}Z\times_{2}Z.

(53)

Let
s_{k}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,k}
be the size of community
k\in[K]
.
We then have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[n],l\in[L]}\Big(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}_{i,j,l}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}_{i,j,l}\Big)^{2}=\sum_{i,j\in[n],l\in[L]}\Big(Z_{i}^{\top}\big(\mathbf{W}_{:,:,l}^{(m)}-\mathbf{W}_{:,:,l}^{(m^{\prime})}\big)Z_{j}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\in[K],l\in[L]}s_{a}s_{b}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a,b,l}^{(m)}-\mathbf{W}_{a,b,l}^{(m^{\prime})}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\in[K],l\in[L]}s_{a}s_{b}\bigg\{\sum_{u=1}^{r}U_{l,u}\ \big(\mathbf{H}_{a,b,u}^{(m)}-\mathbf{H}_{a,b,u}^{(m^{\prime})}\big)\bigg\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b\in[K]}s_{a}s_{b}\delta^{2}\sum_{l\in[L]}\bigg\{\sum_{u=1}^{r}U_{l,u}\big(\mathbf{S}^{(m)}_{a,b,u}-\mathbf{S}^{(m^{\prime})}_{a,b,u}\big)\bigg\}^{2}

where the second equality follows from (
53
),
the fourth equality follows from (
52
) and
the last equality follows from (
50
).

Since (
51
) and
\max_{k\in[K]}s_{k}\leq C_{\sigma}\min_{k\in[K]}s_{k}
, for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma}^{2}\frac{n^{2}}{K^{2}}\lceil L/r\rceil\delta^{2}\sum_{a,b\in[K]}\big\|\mathbf{S}^{(m)}_{a,b,:}-\mathbf{S}^{(m^{\prime})}_{a,b,:}\big\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma}^{2}n^{2}L\delta^{2},

(54)

and

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq C_{\sigma}^{-2}\frac{n^{2}}{K^{2}}\lfloor L/r\rfloor\delta^{2}\sum_{a,b\in[K]}\big\|\mathbf{S}^{(m)}_{a,b,:}-\mathbf{S}^{(m^{\prime})}_{a,b,:}\big\|^{2}\geq C_{\sigma}^{\prime}n^{2}L\delta^{2},

(55)

where the last inequality follows from (
48
) and
C_{\sigma}^{\prime}>0
is an absolute constant.
An identical argument yields that

\displaystyle C_{\sigma}^{\prime}n^{2}L\delta^{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C_{\sigma}^{2}n^{2}L\delta^{2},\quad\forall m\neq m^{\prime}\ \in[M]

(56)

For any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
, combining (
47
), (
54
) and (
56
), the KL divergence between
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}
and
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}
is bounded by

\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}\big)\leq 2\big(2c_{\min}^{-6}+Tc_{\min}^{-3}\big)C_{\sigma}^{2}n^{2}L\delta^{2}\leq C_{1}Tn^{2}L\delta^{2},

where
C_{1}=2(2c_{\min}^{-6}+c_{\min}^{-3})C_{\sigma}^{2}
.

Since
\log(M)\geq c_{2}\log(2)rK^{2}
, choosing

\delta^{2}=\frac{c_{2}\log(2)rK^{2}}{8C_{1}TLn^{2}},

ensures that

\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{m,m^{\prime}\in[M]}\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(0)})}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(0)})}\big)\leq\log(M)/8,

By Fano’s lemma, it follows that

\displaystyle\inf_{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})}\sup_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\Big\{\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}\geq C_{2}\frac{rK^{2}}{T},

(57)

for some constant
C_{2}>0
.

Step 3: Node community construction.

In this step, we construct a lower bound by varying the balanced community membership matrix
Z
, while fixing the connectivity tensors
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{M}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{K\times K\times L}
with
\mbox{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{W})=\mbox{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{M})=1\leq r
.

Fix all layers to share identical two-level connectivity structures

\mathbf{W}_{a,a,l}=p_{1},\quad\forall a\in[K],l\in[L],\quad\mathbf{W}_{a,b,l}=p_{2},\quad\forall a\neq b\in[K],l\in[L]

and

\mathbf{M}_{a,a,l}=p_{3},\quad\forall a\in[K],l\in[L],\quad\mathbf{M}_{a,b,l}=p_{4},\quad\forall a\neq b\in[K],l\in[L],

where
p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]
and
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
,
p_{3}\neq p_{4}
.
Since
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{M}
do not vary across layers, the mode-3 matricizations
\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{W})
and
\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathbf{M})
both have rank
1\leq r
,
satisfying the imposed rank constraint.

Let
\{Z^{(1)},\ldots,Z^{(M)}\}
denote a collection of community membership matrices such that:

•

For the first
\lceil n/2\rceil
nodes, the community assignments are identical across all
\{Z^{(m)}\}_{m\in[M]}
, with each community containing either
\lfloor n/(2K)\rfloor
or
\lceil n/(2K)\rceil
nodes.

•

For the remaining
\lfloor n/2\rfloor
nodes, the community assignments are also balanced and for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
,

d_{H}(Z^{(m)},Z^{(m^{\prime})})\geq c_{3}n/2,

(58)

where
d_{H}(Z,Z^{\prime})=|\{i\in[n]:Z_{i}^{(m)}\neq Z^{(m^{\prime})}_{i}\}|
denotes the Hamming distance between community assignments and
c_{3}>0
is an absolute constant.

By
Lemma

11
, there exists an absolute constant
c_{4}>0
such that

M\geq\exp\big\{2^{-1}c_{4}n\log(K)\big\}.

For each
m\in[M]
, define the corresponding connection probability tensors as

\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}=\mathbf{W}\times_{1}Z^{(m)}\times_{2}Z^{(m)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}=\mathbf{M}\times_{1}Z^{(m)}\times_{2}Z^{(m)}.

Fix
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
and define

\mathcal{S}=\big\{i\in[n]\colon Z_{i}^{(m)}\neq Z_{i}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\}.

For any
k\in[K]
, let

\mathcal{S}^{(m)}_{k}=\big\{i\in\mathcal{S}\colon Z_{i,k}^{(m)}=1\big\},\quad\mathcal{S}^{(m^{\prime})}_{k}=\big\{i\in\mathcal{S}\colon Z_{i,k}^{(m^{\prime})}=1\big\},\quad\mathcal{N}_{k}=\big\{i\notin\mathcal{S}\colon Z_{i,k}^{(m)}=1\big\}.

By the construction of
\{Z^{(m)}\}_{m\in[M]}
and (
58
), there exist absolute constants
c_{4}^{\prime}\geq c_{3}^{\prime}>0
such that

c_{3}n/2\leq|\mathcal{S}|\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor,\quad\max\big\{|\mathcal{S}^{(m)}_{k}|,|\mathcal{S}^{(m^{\prime})}_{k}|\big\}\leq c_{4}^{\prime}\frac{n}{K},\quad\forall k\in[K],

(59)

and

\quad c_{3}^{\prime}\frac{n}{2K}\leq|\mathcal{N}_{k}|<c_{4}^{\prime}\frac{n}{K},\quad\forall k\in[K].

(60)

Define the functions
a,b\colon[n]\to[k]
by

a(i)=\sum_{k\in[K]}kZ_{i,k}^{(m)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad b(i)=\sum_{k\in[K]}kZ_{i,k}^{(m^{\prime})}.

Then we have that

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}+\sum_{l=1}\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}+\sum_{l=1}\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\notin\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}+\sum_{l=1}\sum_{i\notin\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(j),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(j),l}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=(II.1)+(II.2)+(II.3).

(61)

For the term
(II.1)
in (
C.3
), we have that

\displaystyle(II.1)=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{k}}\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),k,l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),k,l}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\{|\mathcal{N}_{a(i)}|\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),a(i),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),a(i),l}\big)^{2}+|\mathcal{N}_{b(i)}|\big(\mathbf{W}_{a(i),b(i),l}-\mathbf{W}_{b(i),b(i),l}\big)^{2}\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\{|\big(\mathcal{N}_{a(i)}|+|\mathcal{N}_{b(i)}|\big)(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\big\}.

(62)

Combining (
59
), (
60
) and (
C.3
), we can derive that

\displaystyle(II.1)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{2c_{4}^{\prime}n}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}=L|\mathcal{S}|\frac{2c_{4}^{\prime}n}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\leq\frac{c_{4}^{\prime}Ln^{2}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}}{K},

(63)

and

\displaystyle(II.1)\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{c_{3}^{\prime}n}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}=L|\mathcal{S}|\frac{c_{3}^{\prime}n}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\geq\frac{c_{3}c_{3}^{\prime}Ln^{2}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}}{2K}.

(64)

Similarly, for the term
(II.2)
in (
C.3
), we have that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{3}c_{3}^{\prime}Ln^{2}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}}{2K}\leq(II.2)\leq\frac{c_{4}^{\prime}Ln^{2}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}}{K}

(65)

For the term
(II.3)
in (
C.3
), we have that

\displaystyle 0\leq(II.3)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\{\mathbbm{1}\{j\in\mathcal{S}_{a(i)}^{(m)},j\notin\mathcal{S}_{b(i)}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\}+\mathbbm{1}\{j\notin\mathcal{S}_{a(i)}^{(m)},j\in\mathcal{S}_{b(i)}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\}\Big\}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\big(\big|\mathcal{S}_{a(i)}^{(m)}\big|+\big|\mathcal{S}_{b(i)}^{(m^{\prime})}\big|\big)(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l\in[L]}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{S}}\frac{2c_{4}^{\prime}n}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\leq\frac{Lc_{4}^{\prime}n^{2}}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2},

(66)

where the third and last inequalities follow from (
59
).

Combining (
C.3
), (
63
), (
64
), (
65
) and (
C.3
), we have that for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
,

C_{3}^{\prime}\frac{Ln^{2}}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}\leq\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C_{3}\frac{Ln^{2}}{K}(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2},

(67)

where
C_{3}\geq C_{3}^{\prime}>0
are absolute constants.
Applying the same arguments, we have that for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
,

C_{3}^{\prime}\frac{Ln^{2}}{K}(p_{3}-p_{4})^{2}\leq\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C_{3}\frac{Ln^{2}}{K}(p_{3}-p_{4})^{2}.

(68)

Combining (
47
), (
67
) and (
68
), the corresponding KL divergence between
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}
and
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}
is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}\big)\leq C_{4}\frac{TLn^{2}}{K}\big\{(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}+(p_{3}-p_{4})^{2}\big\},

where
C_{4}>0
is an absolute constant

Choosing

(p_{1}-p_{2})^{2}=(p_{3}-p_{4})^{2}=\frac{c_{4}nK\log(K)}{16C_{4}TLn^{2}},

yields

\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{m,m^{\prime}\in[M]}\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}\big)\leq\log(M)/8.

By Fano’s lemma, it follows that

\displaystyle\inf_{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})}\sup_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\Big\{\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}\geq C_{4}^{\prime}\frac{n\log(K)}{T}.

(69)

for some absolute constant
C_{4}^{\prime}>0
.

Step 4: Layer construction.

In this step, we construct a lower bound by varying the continuous layer loadings while fixing a balanced community membership matrix
Z
.
Throughout we ensure that
\max\{\mathrm{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{W}),\mathrm{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{M})\}\leq r
and
\mathbf{W},\mathbf{M}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{K\times K\times L}
.

We choose
r
orthonormal columns from the
K^{2}
-dimensional Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{K^{2}}
and reshape them into matrices
\{S^{(s)}\}_{s=1}^{r}\subset\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}
. By construction, we have that

\langle S^{(s)},S^{(t)}\rangle=\sum_{a,b\in[K]}S^{(s)}_{a,b}S^{(t)}_{a,b}=\mathbbm{1}\{s=t\},\quad\forall s,t\in[r],\quad\|S^{(s)}\|_{\infty}\leq 1,\quad\forall s\in[r].

(70)

For any
s\in[r]
, let

A^{(s)}=ZS^{(s)}Z^{\top}

Since

\max_{j\in[K]}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,j}\leq C_{\sigma}\min_{j\in[K]}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i,j},

we obtain that

\langle A^{(s)},A^{(t)}\rangle=0,\quad\forall s\neq t\in[r],\quad\frac{n^{2}}{C_{\sigma}^{2}K^{2}}\leq\|A^{(s)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\ \frac{C_{\sigma}^{2}n^{2}}{K^{2}},\quad\forall s\in[r].

(71)

By the Varshamov–Gilbert lemma, there exist absolute constants
c_{5},c_{6}>0
and a collection
\{V^{(1)},\ldots,V^{(M)}\}\subset\{0,1\}^{L\times r}

such that

\big\|V^{(m)}-V^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq c_{5}Lr,\quad\forall m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M],\quad\mbox{and}\quad M\geq 2^{c_{6}Lr}.

(72)

Let
\alpha>0
be specified later. For any
m\in[M]
, define

\mathbf{W}^{(m)}_{:,:,l}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{K}\mathbf{1}_{K}^{\top}+\alpha\sum_{s=1}^{r}V_{l,s}^{(m)}S^{(s)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{M}^{(m)}_{:,:,l}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{K}\mathbf{1}_{K}^{\top}+\alpha\sum_{s=1}^{r}V_{l,s}^{(m)}S^{(s)},\quad\forall l\in[L].

It follow that
\mathrm{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{W}^{(m)})\leq r
and
\mathrm{rank}_{3}(\mathbf{M}^{(m)})\leq r
.
Moreover, for each
a,b\in[K]
and
l\in[L]

\frac{1}{2}-\alpha r\leq\mathbf{W}^{(m)}_{a,b,l}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\alpha r\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{1}{2}-\alpha r\leq\mathbf{M}^{(m)}_{a,b,l}\leq\frac{1}{2}+\alpha r,

which implies that

\mathbf{W}^{(m)},\mathbf{M}^{(m)}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{K\times K\times L}\quad\mbox{if}\quad\alpha r\leq\frac{1}{2}-c_{\min}.

(73)

For any
m\in[M]
, we then define

\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}=\mathbf{W}^{(m)}\times_{1}Z\times_{2}Z\quad\mbox{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}=\mathbf{M}^{(m)}\times_{1}Z\times_{2}Z,

For any
m\in[M]
and
l\in[L]
, we have that

\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}_{:,:,l}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}+\alpha\sum_{s=1}^{r}V_{l,s}^{(m)}A^{(s)},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}_{:,:,l}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}+\alpha\sum_{s=1}^{r}V_{l,s}^{(m)}A^{(s)}.

(74)

For any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{L}\bigg\|\alpha\sum_{s=1}^{r}\big(V^{(m)}_{l,s}-V^{(m^{\prime})}_{l,s}\big)A^{(s)}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{s=1}^{r}\big(V^{(m)}_{l,s}-V^{(m^{\prime})}_{l,s}\big)^{2}\big\|A^{(s)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(75)

where the first equality follows from (
74
) and the final equality follows from (
71
).
Combining (
71
) and (
C.3
) yields that for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
,

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}{C_{\sigma}^{2}K^{2}}\big\|V^{(m)}-V^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq\frac{c_{5}\alpha^{2}n^{2}Lr}{C_{\sigma}^{2}K^{2}},

(76)

where the last inequality follows from (
72
).
Similarly, for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
, we have that

\displaystyle\big\|\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{C_{\sigma}^{2}\alpha^{2}n^{2}}{K^{2}}\big\|V^{(m)}-V^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{C_{\sigma}^{2}\alpha^{2}n^{2}Lr}{K^{2}}.

(77)

Applying the same arguments, for any
m\neq m^{\prime}\in[M]
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{5}\alpha^{2}n^{2}Lr}{C_{\sigma}^{2}K^{2}}\leq\big\|\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{C_{\sigma}^{2}\alpha^{2}n^{2}Lr}{K^{2}}.

(78)

Combining (
47
), (
77
) and (
78
), the corresponding KL divergence between
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}
and
\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}
is bounded by

\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}\big\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}\big)\leq C_{5}\frac{\alpha^{2}Tn^{2}Lr}{K^{2}}.

for some absolute constant
C_{5}>0
.

Choosing

\alpha^{2}=\frac{c_{6}\log(2)K^{2}}{8C_{5}Tn^{2}},

and using (
73
) together with the assumption
rK\leq c\sqrt{T}n
for some sufficiently small constant
c>0
, for any
m\in[M]
, we have that

\mathbf{W}^{(m)},\mathbf{M}^{(m)}\in[c_{\min},1-c_{\min}]^{K\times K\times L}.

Moreover,

\frac{1}{M^{2}}\sum_{m,m^{\prime}\in[M]}\mathrm{KL}\big(\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m)},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m)})}\|\mathcal{P}_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta}^{(m^{\prime})},\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(m^{\prime})})}\big)\leq\log(M)/8.

By Fano’s lemma, it follows that

\displaystyle\inf_{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})}\sup_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\Big\{\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}\geq C_{6}\frac{Lr}{T}.

(79)

for some absolute constant
C_{6}>0
.

Step 5.
Combining (
57
), (
69
) and (
79
), we finally obtain that there exists an absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

\inf_{(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}})}\sup_{(\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta})\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{\Theta},\boldsymbol{\Delta}}\Big\{\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\big\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Big\}\geq C^{\prime}\frac{rK^{2}+n\log(K)+Lr}{T},

which completes the proof.

∎

C.4
Proof of Proposition
3

Proof of
Proposition

3
.

Let

O_{K}^{t}=\arginf_{O\in\mathbb{O}_{K\times K}}\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O\|.

Recall that
U_{Z}
is defined (
25
). For any
i\in[n]
, let
\gamma_{i}^{t}
and
\widehat{\gamma}_{i}^{t}
denote the
i
th rows of

U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}
and
\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}
, respectively.
Let
\{\tilde{u}^{(t,k)}\}_{k=1}^{K}
denote the
K
distinct row patterns of
\widetilde{U}^{t}
. For any node
i\in[n]
, denote its true community by
z(i)\in[K]
and its assigned community at time
t\in[T]
by
\hat{z}^{t}(i)\in[K]
. For any
k\in[K]
, let
\mathcal{V}_{k}=\{i\in[n]\colon z(i)=k\}
.

For any
i,j\in[n]
, we have that

\|\gamma_{i}^{t}-\gamma_{j}^{t}\|_{2}=\|Z_{i}D_{Z}^{-1}O_{K}^{t}-Z_{j}D_{Z}^{-1}O_{K}^{t}\|_{2}=\|Z_{i}D_{Z}^{-1}-Z_{j}D_{Z}^{-1}\|_{2}=\begin{cases}0,&\mbox{if }Z_{i}=Z_{j},\\
\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{s_{\max}}},&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus, there are exactly
K
distinct rows in
U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}
, say

\{\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}\}_{k=1}^{K}
, and
U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{n,K}
. By
1
(ii)
, for any
k\neq l\in[K]
, we have that

\|\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}-\gamma_{l}^{t,\ast}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{s_{\max}}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}.

(80)

Define the event

\mathcal{A}=\Big\{\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sqrt{K}\mathcal{E}_{t},\forall t\in[T]\Big\},

(81)

where
\mathcal{E}_{t}
is defined in (
27
). By
Proposition

10
,
\mathcal{A}
holds with probability at least
1-(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c}
. From now on, we assume the event
\mathcal{A}
holds.

Step 1: Misclassification error bound.

Define

\mathcal{J}=\bigg\{i\in[n]\colon\|\widehat{\gamma}_{i}^{t}-\gamma_{i}^{t}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{9}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}\bigg\}.

(82)

Then, from (
81
),

|\mathcal{J}^{c}|\frac{2K}{81C_{\sigma}n}\leq\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}^{c}}\|\widehat{\gamma}_{i}^{t}-\gamma_{i}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq K\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2},

Hence, it holds that

|\mathcal{J}^{c}|\leq\frac{81C_{\sigma}n\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2}}{2}.

(83)

Step 1.1: Existence and uniqueness of nearest cluster centers.

In this sub-step, we aim to show that for each
k\in[K]
, there exists a unique
k^{\prime}\in[K]
and

\big\|\tilde{u}^{(t,k^{\prime})}-\gamma_{k}^{\ast}\big\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{3}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}.

We first prove the existence. Suppose instead that for some
k\in[K]
,

\big\|\tilde{u}^{(t,k^{\prime})}-\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}\big\|_{2}>\frac{1}{3}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}\quad\mbox{for all }k^{\prime}\in[K].

In particular, for any
j\in\mathcal{V}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}
, the center assigned to
j
is
\tilde{u}^{(t,\hat{z}^{t}(j))}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\tilde{u}^{(t,\hat{z}^{t}(j))}\|_{2}\geq

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}^{(t,\hat{z}^{t}(j))}-\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}\|_{2}-\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}^{(t,\hat{z}^{t}(j))}-\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}\|_{2}-\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}-\frac{1}{9}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}=\frac{2}{9}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}},

where the first equality follows from
j\in\mathcal{V}_{k}
, and second inequality follows from
j\in\mathcal{J}
and (
82
).

By
1
(ii)
, each community
k\in[K]
satisfies

|\mathcal{V}_{k}|\geq C_{\sigma}^{-1}n/K
. By (
83
), we obtain that

|\mathcal{V}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}|=|\mathcal{V}_{k}|-|\mathcal{V}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}^{c}|\geq\frac{n}{C_{\sigma}K}-\frac{81C_{\sigma}n\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2}}{2}\geq\frac{n}{2C_{\sigma}K}.

where the last inequality follows from (
8
).
Then, by the definition of
\widetilde{U}^{t}
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq|\mathcal{V}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}|\cdot\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\tilde{u}^{(t,\hat{z}^{t}(j))}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{n}{2C_{\sigma}K}\frac{8K}{81C_{\sigma}n}=\frac{4}{81C_{\sigma}^{2}}.

(84)

Combining (
81
) and (
C.4
) yields that

\frac{4}{81C_{\sigma}^{2}}\leq\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq K\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2}

This contradicts (
8
).
Thus, we prove the existence.

We now prove the uniqueness. By (
80
), any two distinct population centers
\gamma_{k}^{\ast}
and
\gamma_{l}^{\ast}
with
k\neq l
are separated by at least
\sqrt{2K/(C_{\sigma}n)}
.
Therefore, no single empirical center
\tilde{u}^{(t,k^{\prime})}
can lie within distance
\sqrt{2K/(C_{\sigma}n)}/3
of two different population centers simultaneously.
This establishes the uniqueness.

Step 1.2: Correct classification of nodes in
\mathcal{J}
.

For each
k\in[K]
, we relabel
\tilde{u}^{(t,k)}
according to its nearest population center
\gamma_{k^{\prime}}^{\ast}
established in
Step 1.1
.

For any
j\in\mathcal{V}_{k}\cap J
, we have that

\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\tilde{u}^{(t,k))}\|_{2}\leq\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}\|_{2}+\|\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}-\tilde{u}^{(t,k)}\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{9}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}+\frac{1}{3}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}\leq\frac{4}{9}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}},

where the second inequality follows from
j\in\mathcal{J}
and (
82
).
For any
l\neq k
, we have that

\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\tilde{u}^{(t,l)}\|\geq\|\gamma_{k}^{t,\ast}-\gamma_{l}^{t,\ast}\|-\|\gamma_{l}^{\ast}-\tilde{u}^{(t,l)}\|\geq\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}-\frac{1}{3}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}\geq\frac{2}{3}\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}.

Since

\frac{4}{9}<\frac{2}{3},

each
\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}
with
j\in\mathcal{V}_{k}\cap\mathcal{J}
is closer to
\tilde{u}^{(t,k)}
than to any
\tilde{u}^{(t,l)}
for
l\neq k
.
Thus all nodes in
\mathcal{J}
are correctly clustered, and any misclassification can only occur in

\mathcal{J}^{c}
.

Therefore, using (
83
),

\mathcal{L}(\widehat{Z}^{t},Z)=n^{-1}|\mathcal{J}^{c}|\leq n^{-1}\frac{81C_{\sigma}n\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2}}{2}\leq\frac{81C_{\sigma}}{2}\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2},

which proves (
9
).

Step 2. Exact recovery.

Step 2.1.

We first show that
Z_{i}\neq Z_{j}
implies
\widehat{Z}_{i}^{t}\neq\widehat{Z}_{j}^{t}
.

Suppose, for contradiction, that there exist
i,j\in[n]
such that
Z_{i}\neq Z_{j}
but
\widehat{Z}_{i}^{t}=\widehat{Z}_{j}^{t}
, which implies that
\widetilde{U}^{t}_{i}=\widetilde{U}^{t}_{j}
.
By the definition of
\widetilde{U}^{t}
and
U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{n,K}
,

\|U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}\\
\leq 2\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 2K\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2}.

(85)

On the other hand, we have that

\|U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}\geq\|\gamma_{i}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\gamma_{j}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{j}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\|\gamma_{i}^{t}-\gamma_{j}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}.

(86)

Combining (
80
), (
85
) and (
86
) yields that

\sqrt{\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}}\leq\|U_{Z}O_{K}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2}\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-U_{Z}O_{K}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2K}\mathcal{E}_{t},

contradicting (
10
).
Hence
Z_{i}\neq Z_{j}
implies
\widehat{Z}_{i}^{t}\neq\widehat{Z}_{j}^{t}
.

Step 2.2.

Next we show that
Z_{i}=Z_{j}
implies
\widehat{Z}_{i}^{t}=\widehat{Z}_{j}^{t}
.

Assume instead that there exist
i\neq j\in[n]
such that
Z_{i}=Z_{j}
but
\widehat{Z}_{i}^{t}\neq\widehat{Z}_{j}^{t}
, which implies

\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}\neq\widetilde{U}_{j}^{t}
.
From
Step 1
,
\widetilde{U}^{t}
has exactly
K
distinct rows, each corresponding to a true community.
Thus, because
\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}\neq\widetilde{U}_{j}^{t}
but
Z_{i}=Z_{j}
, there must exist some
k\neq i,j
such that
Z_{i}=Z_{j}\neq Z_{k}
and
\widetilde{U}_{j}^{t}=\widetilde{U}_{k}^{t}
.

Construct
\widetilde{U}^{t,*}
by replacing the
j
th row of
\widetilde{U}^{t}

with
\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-\widetilde{U}^{t,*}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{k}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{j}^{t}+\gamma_{i}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{j}^{t}+\gamma_{i}^{t}-\gamma_{k}^{t}+\gamma_{k}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{k}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{j}^{t}+\gamma_{i}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{i}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\widehat{\gamma}_{j}^{t}-\gamma_{j}^{t}+\gamma_{k}^{t}-\widetilde{U}_{k}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\gamma_{i}^{t}-\gamma_{k}^{t}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}+2\|U_{Z}O_{K}^{t}-\widetilde{U}^{t}\|_{F}^{2}-\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}

\displaystyle\leq 4K\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2}-\frac{2K}{C_{\sigma}n}

\displaystyle<0,

a contradiction. Therefore
Z_{i}=Z_{j}
implies
\widehat{Z}_{i}^{t}=\widehat{Z}_{j}^{t}
.

Combining
Steps 2.1
and
2.2
, we conclude that under (
10
),
\widehat{Z}^{t}
exactly recovers the clustering structure encoded by
Z
, completing the proof of (
11
).

∎

C.5
Proofs for Appendix
C.1

This section provides proofs of the results stated in Appendix
C.1
. The proofs are organized as follows: Proposition
8
is proved in Appendix
C.5.1
, Lemma
9
in Appendix
C.5.2
,
Proposition

10
in Appendix
C.5.3
, Lemma
11
in Appendix
C.5.4
and Lemma
12
in Appendix
C.5.5
.

C.5.1
Proof of Proposition
8

Proof of Proposition
8
.

By
Lemma

7
, for any
1\leq i\leq j\leq n
and
l\in[L]
, the sequence
\{\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is strongly mixing. Since strong mixing is preserved under measurable transformations, any process constructed from finitely many such variables is also strongly mixing. In particular, the process

\big\{\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t}(1-\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{t-1})\big\}_{t\geq 1}

is strongly mixing.
For any
t\in[T]
, define

\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t}=t^{-1}\sum_{u=1}^{t}\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}=t^{-1}\sum_{u=1}^{t}\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u}(1-\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u-1}).

Let

\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l}=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{0}\},\quad\forall 1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L].

By stationarity (
Proposition

6
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}=t^{-1}\sum_{u=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u-1}\big\}=\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}=

\displaystyle t^{-1}\sum_{u=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u}(1-\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u-1})\big\}\Big\}=(1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l})\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}.

Applying the Bernstein inequality for
\alpha
-mixing sequences
(
Merlevède et al. 2009
, e.g. Theorem 1 in)
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}\big|\geq\varepsilon\big\}\leq\exp\bigg\{-\frac{c_{0}t\varepsilon^{2}}{1+\varepsilon\log(t)\log\log(t)}\bigg\},

(87)

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t,k}\big\}\big|\geq\varepsilon\big\}\leq\exp\bigg\{-\frac{c_{0}t\varepsilon^{2}}{1+\varepsilon\log(t)\log\log(t)}\bigg\},

(88)

where
C_{0},c_{0}>0
are absolute constants.

Let

\mathcal{A}=\Big\{\max\big\{\big|\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t,k}-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t,k}\big\}\big|,\big|\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t,k}-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t,k}\big\}\big|\big\}\leq\varepsilon\Big\}.

Using a union bound argument with (
87
) and (
88
), we can derive that

\mathbb{P}\{\mathcal{A}\}\geq 1-2\exp\bigg\{-\frac{c_{0}\varepsilon^{2}}{1+\varepsilon\log(t)\log\log(t)}\bigg\}.

(89)

Note that

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}=\frac{t^{-1}\sum_{u=1}^{t}\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u}(1-\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u-1})}{t^{-1}\sum_{u=1}^{t}(1-\mathbf{A}_{i,j,l}^{u-1})}=\frac{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}}{1-\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t}}.

Under
\mathcal{A}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}-\varepsilon}{1-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}+\varepsilon}\leq\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}+\varepsilon}{1-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}-\varepsilon}.

(90)

Next, still under the event
\mathcal{A}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\frac{\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}^{t}\big\}-\varepsilon}{1-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}^{t,k}\big\}+\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\frac{(1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l})\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}-\varepsilon}{(1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l})+\varepsilon}=\frac{\varepsilon(1+\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l})}{1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l}+\varepsilon}\leq\varepsilon\frac{(1+c_{\min})}{c_{\min}}.

(91)

Similarly, for any
\varepsilon\leq c_{\min}/2
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{Y}_{i,j,l}\big\}+\varepsilon}{1-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbf{X}_{i,j,l}\big\}-\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l})\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}+\varepsilon}{1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l}-\varepsilon}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}=\frac{\varepsilon(1+\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l})}{1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l}-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon(1+\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l})}{1-\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{i,j,l}-c_{\min}/2}\leq\varepsilon\frac{2(1+c_{\min})}{c_{\min}}.

(92)

Combining (
89
), (
90
), (
91
) and (
C.5.1
), we conclude that

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq C_{0}\varepsilon\Big\}\leq 2\exp\bigg\{-\frac{c_{0}t\varepsilon^{2}}{1+\varepsilon\log(t)\log\log(t)}\bigg\},

(93)

where
C_{0}>0
is an absolute constant.
Similarly, we have that

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq C_{0}\varepsilon\Big\}\leq 2\exp\bigg\{-\frac{c_{0}t\varepsilon^{2}}{1+\varepsilon\log(t)\log\log(t)}\bigg\},

Taking

\varepsilon=\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}},

and by a union bound argument, we have that

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|+\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq 2C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}\Big\}\leq 4\exp\bigg\{-\frac{c_{0}\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}\log\log(t)}\bigg\}

Since
t\{\log(t)\log\log(t)\}^{-2}\gtrsim\log(n\vee L\vee T)
, we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|+\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}\Big\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{1}}

where
C_{1}>0
and
c_{1}>3
. By a union bound argument, there exists an absolute constant
c_{2}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|+\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}\big|>C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}},\forall 1\leq i\leq j\leq n,l\in[L]\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{2}}.

Let

u_{0}=\frac{C_{0}}{\log(t)\log\log(t)}.

For any
q\geq 1
, by integration by parts, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|^{q}\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}qu^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq u\big\}du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u_{0}}qu^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq u\big\}du+\int_{u_{0}}^{\infty}qu^{q-1}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}\big|\geq u\big\}du

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{u_{0}}qu^{q-1}\exp\Big\{-\frac{c_{0}t}{2C_{0}^{2}}u^{2}\Big\}du+2\int_{u_{0}}^{\infty}qu^{q-1}\exp\Big\{-\frac{c_{0}t}{2C_{0}\log(t)\log\log(t)}u\Big\}du

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I.1)+(I.2),

(94)

where the first inequality follows from (
93
).

For the term
(I.1)
in (
C.5.1
), let
\kappa=\sqrt{c_{0}t/(2C_{0}^{2})}
and substitute
s=\kappa u
. Then

\displaystyle(I.1)=

\displaystyle 2q\kappa^{-q}\int_{0}^{\kappa u_{0}}s^{q-1}e^{-s^{2}}ds\leq 2q\kappa^{-q}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{q-1}e^{-s^{2}}ds=q\kappa^{-q}\Gamma(q/2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q\bigg(\frac{2C_{0}^{2}}{c_{0}t}\bigg)^{q/2}\Gamma(q/2)\leq\bigg(C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{q}{t}}\bigg)^{q},

(95)

for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
.
For the term
(I.2)
in (
C.5.1
), let
\lambda=c_{0}t/\{2C_{0}\log(t)\log\log(t)\}
and substitute
s=\lambda u
. Then

\displaystyle(I.2)=

\displaystyle 2q\lambda^{-q}\int_{\lambda u_{0}}^{\infty}s^{q-1}e^{-s}ds=2q\lambda^{-q}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{q-1}e^{-s}ds=2q\lambda^{-q}\Gamma(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2q\bigg(\frac{2C_{0}\log(t)\log\log(t)}{c_{0}t}\bigg)^{q}\Gamma(q)\leq\bigg(C_{3}\frac{q\log(t)\log\log(t)}{C_{0}t}\bigg)^{q},

(96)

for some absolute constant
C_{3}>0
.
Combining (
C.5.1
), (
C.5.1
) and (
C.5.1
) yields that

\displaystyle\big[\mathbb{E}\big\{|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,k,l}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,k,l}|^{q}\big\}\big]^{1/q}\leq C_{4}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{q}{t}}+\frac{q\log(t)\log\log(t)}{C_{0}t}\bigg),

for some absolute constant
C_{4}>0
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{q\geq 1}\frac{\big[\mathbb{E}\big\{|\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,k,l}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,k,l}|^{q}\big\}\big]^{1/q}}{q}\leq C_{4}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{1}{t}}+\frac{\log(t)\log\log(t)}{C_{0}t}\bigg)\leq C_{5}t^{-1/2},

(97)

where
C_{5}>0
is an absolute constant and the last inequality uses the fact
C^{\prime}t\geq\{\log(t)\log\log(t)\}^{2}
for any
t\geq 1
and some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
. By Proposition 2.6.1 in
Vershynin 2018
, it follows that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}
is
C_{6}t^{-1/2}
-sub-Gaussian distributed for some absolute constant
C_{6}>0
. An analogous argument applies to
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}
.
These complete the proof.
∎

C.5.2
Proof of Lemma
9

Proof of
Lemma

9
.

Each row of
Z
contains exactly one
1
and each column
k\in[K]
contains
s_{k}
ones, so

Z^{\top}Z=\mathrm{diag}(s_{1},\dots,s_{K}).

Therefore
\mathrm{rank}(Z)=K
,
\sigma_{1}(Z)=\sqrt{s_{\max}}
and

\sigma_{K}(Z)=\sqrt{s_{\min}}
.

By the definition and basic properties of tensor matricisation
(
Zhang and Xia 2018
, e.g. Lemma 4 in )
, we see that

\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})=Z\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})(Z\otimes I_{L})^{\top}.

We aim to show that
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega}))=\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q}))
. According to Lemma S3 in the Supplement of
Wang et al. 2025
, it suffices to verify that
Z\otimes I_{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{nL\times KL}
has full column rank, i.e.
\mathrm{rank}(Z\otimes I_{L})=KL
.
Using the property of Kronecker products, we obtain
\mathrm{rank}(Z\otimes I_{L})=\mathrm{rank}(Z)\mathrm{rank}(I_{L})=LK
.

Next, we have that

\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\|\leq\|Z\|\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})\|\|Z\otimes I_{L}\|=\sigma_{1}^{2}(Z)\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})\|\leq s_{\max}\|\mathbf{Q}\|.

It follows from Lemma S4 in the Supplement of
Wang et al. 2025
that

\sigma_{\min}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega}))\geq\sigma_{\min}(Z)\sigma_{\min}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q}))\sigma_{\min}(Z\otimes I_{L})=\sigma_{K}^{2}(Z)\sigma_{K}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q}))\geq s_{\min}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{Q}).

Applying the same arguments, we obtain for
s\in[3]
that

\mathrm{rank}\big(\mathcal{M}_{s}(\mathbf{\Omega}\big)=\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{M}_{s}(\mathbf{Q}))

and

s_{\min}\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{Q})\leq\sigma_{\min}\big(\mathcal{M}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\Omega})\big)\leq\big\|\mathcal{M}_{s}(\boldsymbol{\Omega})\big\|\leq s_{\max}\|\mathbf{Q}\|.

We complete the proof.

∎

C.5.3
Proof of Proposition
10

Proof of Proposition
10
.

By Lemma 1 in
Cai and Zhang 2018
, it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{O\in\mathbb{O}_{K\times K}}\|\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z}-U_{Z}O\|\leq\sqrt{2}\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{U}^{t}_{Z},U_{Z})\|.

(98)

For any
t\in[T]
and
k\in[t]
, define

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{t}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{t}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{t}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\Omega}=\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}.

(99)

Let
\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}}=\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}}-\mathbf{\Omega}
, where
\tilde{t}=2^{\lfloor\log_{2}(t)\rfloor}
. Note that
\tilde{t}\leq t<2\tilde{t}
.

By Theorem 3 in
Zhang et al. 2022
and
Lemma

9
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\big\|\sin\Theta\big(\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t},U_{Z}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big)\big\|}{\sigma_{K}^{2}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\frac{\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big)\big\|}{s_{\min}^{2}\sigma_{K}^{2}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})\big)},

(100)

where
\mathbf{Q}
is defined in
1
and the last inequality follows from
lemma

9
.

Moreover,

\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}=\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}+\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}+\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top})\big)\big\|+2\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})^{\top})\big)\big\|+4\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{\tilde{t}})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I)+(II),

(101)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma 3 in the Supplement of
Zhang et al. 2022
.

Step 1.
In this step, we aim to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(I)>C^{\prime}\bigg(\frac{n\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}+\frac{n^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg)\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c^{\prime}},

(102)

where
C^{\prime},c^{\prime}>0
are absolute constants.

Note that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Omega}}^{\tilde{t}}=\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}}
and
\boldsymbol{\Omega}=\boldsymbol{\Theta}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}
. By the triangle inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\}\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\mathrm{diag}\big((\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}))(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}))^{\top}\big)\bigg\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\bigg\|\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\}\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\mathrm{diag}\big((\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}))(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}))^{\top}\big)\bigg\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\bigg\|\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\}\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\mathrm{diag}\big((\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}))(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}))^{\top}\big)\bigg\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\bigg\|\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\}\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\mathrm{diag}\big((\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}))(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}))^{\top}\big)\bigg\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I.1)+(I.2)+(I.3)+(I.4).

(103)

We now focus on the term
(I.1)
in (
C.5.3
). Let

X^{\tilde{t}}=\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times nL}.

By
Proposition

8
, each entry of
X^{\tilde{t}}
is
C^{*}t^{-1/2}
-sub-Gaussian distributed for some absolute constant
C^{*}>0
.

Then we have that

\displaystyle(I.1)=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\big((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big)\Big\}\bigg\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}-\mathrm{diag}\Big((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+E\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}-\mathrm{diag}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big)\Big\}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}-\mathrm{diag}\Big((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big)\Big\}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}-\mathrm{diag}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big)\Big\}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big\}\bigg\|+\bigg\|\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\mathrm{diag}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big)\Big\}\bigg\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I.1.1)+(I.1.2),

(104)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma 3 in the Supplement of
Zhang et al. 2022
.

For the term
(I.1.1)
in (
C.5.3
), the matrix Bernstein inequality
(
Vershynin 2018
, e.g. Theorem 5.4.1 in)
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(I.1.1)>C_{1}\max\bigg\{\frac{n\sqrt{L\log(n\vee L\vee T)}}{t},\frac{n\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c_{1}},

(105)

where
C_{1},c_{1}>0
are absolute constants.
For the term
(I.1.2)
in (
C.5.3
), we have that

\displaystyle(I.1.2)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}\}^{\top}-\mathrm{diag}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big\{(X^{\tilde{t}})^{j}((X^{\tilde{t}})^{j})^{\top}\big\}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{nL}\sqrt{\sum_{i,k=1}^{n}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\{X^{\tilde{t}}_{i,j}X^{\tilde{t}}_{k,j}\}\Big\}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\{X^{\tilde{t}}_{i,j}X^{\tilde{t}}_{i,j}\}\Big\}^{2}}.

(106)

By Lemma 18 in
Jiang et al. 2023
, there exists an absolute constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\max\big\{|\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}\}-\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{i,j,l}|,|\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{i,j,l}^{t}\}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{i,j,l}|\big\}\leq C_{2}t^{-1},\forall i,j\in[n],l\in[L],t\in[T].

(107)

Combining (
C.5.3
) and (
107
) yields that

\displaystyle(I.1.2)\leq\frac{C_{2}^{2}n^{2}L}{t^{2}}.

(108)

Putting together (
C.5.3
), (
105
) and (
108
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(I.1)>C_{1}\frac{n\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}+C_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{1}}.

Similar arguments apply to
(I.2)
,
(I.3)
and
(I.4)
in (
C.5.3
).
Therefore, by a union bound argument, we derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(I)>4C_{1}\frac{n\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}+4C_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg\}\leq 4(n\vee L\vee T)^{-c_{1}},

(109)

which proves (
102
).

Step 2
. In this step, we aim to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(II)>C^{\prime\prime}\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big\|\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{n\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+\frac{\sqrt{n^{2}L}}{t}\bigg)\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c^{\prime\prime}}

(110)

where
C^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime}>0
are absolute constants.

Note that

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle 4\big\|\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})+\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\big\}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\Big\|\Big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\}\Big\}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+4\Big\|\Big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\big\}\Big\}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+4\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+4\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\big\}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\Big\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(II.1)+(II.2)+(II.3)+(II.4).

(111)

By Lemma 2 in the Supplement of
Zhang et al. 2022
and
Proposition

8
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(II.1)+(II.2)>C_{3}\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big\|\sqrt{\frac{n\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c_{3}},

(112)

where
C_{3},c_{3}>0
are absolute constants. We also have that

\displaystyle(II.3)+(II.4)\leq

\displaystyle 4\Big[\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Theta})\big\}\Big\|+\Big\|\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathcal{M}_{1}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}^{\tilde{t}})-\mathcal{M}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\Delta})\big\}\Big\|\Big]\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8C_{2}\frac{n\sqrt{L}}{t}\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})^{\top}\big\|

(113)

where the last inequality follows from (
107
).
Combining (
C.5.3
), (
112
) and (
C.5.3
) yields for some absolute constant
C_{4}>0
that

\mathbb{P}\bigg\{(II)>C_{4}\big\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{\Omega})\big\|\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{n\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+\frac{\sqrt{n^{2}L}}{t}\bigg\}\leq(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c_{3}}

which proves (
110
).

Step 3.

Combining (
98
), (
C.5.3
), (
C.5.3
), (
102
) and (
110
) yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[

\displaystyle\inf_{O\in\mathbb{O}_{K\times K}}\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-U_{Z}O\|>C_{4}\bigg\{\frac{n\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{ts_{\min}^{2}\sigma_{K}^{2}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})\big)}+\frac{n^{2}L}{t^{2}s_{\min}^{2}\sigma_{K}^{2}\big(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q})\big)}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{s_{\max}\sigma_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q}))}{s_{\min}^{2}\sigma_{K}^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathbf{Q}))}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{n\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{t}}+\frac{n\sqrt{L}}{t}\bigg)\bigg\}\leq 2(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c_{4}},

where
C_{4},c_{4}>0
are absolute constants. Under
1
, we finally obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[

\displaystyle\inf_{O\in\mathbb{O}_{K\times K}}\|\widehat{U}_{Z}^{t}-U_{Z}O\|>C_{5}\sigma_{Q}^{-2}\bigg(\frac{K^{2}\sqrt{L}\log^{2}(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}+\frac{K^{2}L}{t^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+C_{5}\sigma_{Q}^{-1}\bigg(K\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee L\vee T)}{tn}}+\frac{K\sqrt{L}}{t}\bigg)\bigg\}\leq 2(n\vee L\vee t)^{-c_{4}},

where
C_{5}>0
is an absolute constant. We complete the proof.
